#5191. Core inflation excludes oil. Bitcoin investors still cannot ignore it

Stronger post-2020 oil transmission could complicate monetary easing, but second-round effects have not yet appeared.

Oil could remain an obstacle to lower borrowing costs and greater risk appetite among Bitcoin investors, even when energy is excluded from core inflation. A Federal Reserve staff study published Nov. 9 estimates a stronger link between oil-price changes and underlying inflation in data from 2021 onward, potentially complicating monetary easing.

The findings put numbers to a channel behind the financing risks discussed in CryptoSlate's recent diesel coverage. They describe a conditional inflation threat: the authors say second-round effects from the current oil shock have not yet materialized.

Excluding energy does not remove every fuel cost embedded in other products. The U.K.'s Office for National Statistics explains that core CPI strips out direct energy components while retaining indirect energy costs in prices such as hospitality, telecommunications and transportation. Businesses can therefore pass energy costs into prices outside the excluded categories.

The Fed researchers pooled Canada, the euro area, Japan, the United Kingdom and the United States, using data from 2000 through the second quarter of 2026. The U.S. measure is core personal consumption expenditures inflation; the others use core consumer price inflation.

For the period beginning in 2021, the model links a 10-percentage-point increase in relative oil-price inflation to a roughly 0.14-percentage-point increase in core inflation. In the pre-2021 period, the estimated response was about 0.01 percentage point and statistically insignificant.

Those units matter. Relative oil-price inflation measures quarterly changes in local-currency Brent prices relative to core inflation. A 10-percentage-point increase in that measure is different from a 10% rise in oil's price level. The model allows transmission through the current quarter and four previous quarters.

Energy components are excluded, while indirect costs can remain. The staff model estimates a stronger oil-to-core link in the period beginning in 2021.

The earlier sample's estimated response was about 0.01 percentage point and statistically insignificant.

The later sample's estimated response was about 0.14 percentage point. This is a pooled model estimate, not a forecast.

Persistent core pressure could delay financing relief for Bitcoin investors; the Bitcoin link remains an inference.

Forward paths are upper bounds, not official forecasts; the authors say current-shock second-round effects have not yet appeared. Dashed connector marks the inferred Bitcoin channel, not an observed response.

Dashed connector marks the inferred Bitcoin channel, not an observed response.

The Fed authors call their forward inflation paths an upper bound rather than a central prediction, assuming elevated persistence and pass-through continue through 2028. Their pooled estimates may also capture wider post-pandemic shocks rather than a lasting change in oil's influence. These are staff findings, not official U.S. inflation forecasts.

In a separate structural simulation, the Fed staff find that stronger oil transmission combined with more persistent inflation requires materially tighter policy even as activity weakens.

That creates a possible headwind for Bitcoin through investors' willingness to take risk. An August 2023 IMF working paper found that U.S. monetary tightening reduced a common crypto-price factor through this risk-taking channel.

The Bitcoin implication is an inference from the two studies: if oil sustains core-price pressure, it could delay financing relief and constrain risk appetite. The studies do not estimate a Bitcoin price response.

The BLS schedule lists the October CPI report for Nov. 10. Persistent core pressure accompanied by reduced easing expectations would support the financing-risk interpretation; sustained disinflation and improving easing expectations would weaken it.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Avalanche [AVAX] wasn’t having a great time. Then, NYSE decided to use Avalanche to settle tokenized stocks, and AVAX woke up fast!

The timing worked out nicely too, with the platform walking out of its best quarter yet.

To put it simply, Avalanche is where the receipts will get written.

According to a press release, Avalanche will be the core ledger for the clearing firm behind an upcoming NYSE-affiliated trading platform. Every trade will get cleared and settled on it.

The stocks themselves can be issued on other blockchains.

NYSE’s own Pillar engine will still handle the trading. The plan is round-the-clock trading, instant settlement and funding with stablecoins, though it’s pending regulator approval.

Why Avalanche though? According to Dennis Lee, the man who heads digital issuance at the NYSE, the exchange wanted a ledger that settles fast, keeps costs predictable, and lets them decide who gets in.

The timing here is interesting because this NYSE news is coming along the back of Avalanche’s best Q3 record.

According to data from Blockworks and Avalanche, Pharaoh’s DEX volume went up by 2x from Q2 to Q3 to $5.33 billion. Spending on Avalanche-linked cards also went up to 74%.

For anyone wondering if people actually want stocks on Avalanche, here’s something to note though. Dinari’s stock purchases went up 34% to 9 million. That’s small next to Wall Street, yes, but it’s the same idea the NYSE is now building towards. Just… bigger.

On top of all that, the mainnet has handled 1.6 billion transactions in total!

The altcoin spent most of the morning simply drifting around $8.00, after bottoming out near $7.83. From there, it went up fast.

In fact, its price shot up from $8.10 to $8.45, and it didn’t stop. AVAX tagged roughly $8.93 before settling near $8.83. That hinted at a 12.7% hike above the altcoin’s daily low.

Its RSI shot past 90 during the spike, before falling to 62.5 at press time. That was just below the Signal Line.

Finally, the DMI seemed to be bit more convincing. The ADX was at 56.7, hinting at a strong trend. Buyers seemed to be leading the charge, with +DI at 29.7 against -DI at 13.1.

If AVAX can push through $9, things are likely to get interesting.

The treasury backed with nearly 500 million XRP tokens uncovers the true reality behind XRP’s RLUSD relation.

Evernorth, the XRP-only treasury debuting on Wall Street, just published a data-backed report claiming that XRP was used in 92% of RLUSD-tied swaps. That’s the count for 2026 across all trades on the XRP Ledger’s native decentralized exchange (DEX).

That statement differs a lot from the speculation on social media in regards to XRP’s “displaced” status in the ecosystem since RLUSD’s arrival. Ripple’s native stablecoin quickly gained public traction and rose to a $2 billion market cap in less than two months.

It’s crucial to understand that $1.65 billion represents RLUSD’s swap volume on the XRP Ledger’s built-in order-book exchange, rather than the stablecoin’s total circulation across networks. Those figures are considerably larger and don’t necessarily use XRP.

However, the 92% slice on Ripple’s native decentralized exchange vividly paints XRP’s direct involvement in compliance-first stablecoin-dominated markets. That implies the stablecoin’s rapid expansion is accompanied by XRP’s demand, not the opposite.

Evernorth’s own NASDAQ debut in October came with 473 million XRP tokens, pursuing active treasury management with expectations to expand towards 1.8 billion XRP coins. The debut on traditional stock markets served investors a chance to gain exposure to XRP indirectly.

#5187. Bitcoin Faces a Tougher Rate Outlook After US October Inflation Rises 0.4%

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

The latest US inflation report has given Bitcoin traders a fresh reason to watch the bond market rather than just the crypto charts. The Bureau of Labor Statistics reported on November 10 that consumer prices rose 0.4% in October, above the 0.3% monthly increase economists had broadly expected. Core prices, which exclude food and energy, increased 0.3%. The headline figure slowed from September’s 0.6% increase but still points to inflation pressures that the Federal Reserve cannot easily ignore.

TL;DR: October US CPI rose 0.4% month on month and core CPI increased 0.3%. The hotter reading strengthened expectations of tighter monetary policy. For Bitcoin, the immediate transmission mechanism runs through interest-rate expectations, the dollar, liquidity and risk appetite—not a mechanical relationship between CPI and BTC.

A quarter-point difference in inflation might sound small, yet markets were positioned around a particular expectation. When the result is higher than forecast, investors must reconsider how soon the Fed can relax policy, or whether further tightening is required. Higher Treasury yields can make cash and government securities more attractive relative to assets that offer no contractual income, including Bitcoin.

The BLS report is also unusual because it does not include a year-on-year October CPI rate. Data-collection disruptions during the federal shutdown in October 2025 left the agency without the normal comparison base. Readers should not fill that gap with an invented annual figure or compare an unofficial estimate with an official monthly release. Shelter costs increased 0.3% and accounted for more than 30% of October’s monthly rise, while food increased 0.5% and energy 0.7%.

Bitcoin does not have a fixed reaction function to inflation. Some investors describe it as protection against monetary debasement, while others trade it as a high-volatility risk asset. Both descriptions can influence positioning, sometimes on the same day. A surprise that pushes real yields higher can pressure speculative positions even when the longer-run inflation-hedge narrative remains intact.

The distinction is especially relevant after recent fund withdrawals. Our report on November 6 Bitcoin ETF outflows explains why heavy selling through listed funds can complicate a market already reacting to macro news. ETF redemptions, however, are not the same thing as every Bitcoin holder exiting.

There is a corporate dimension too. Firms that finance Bitcoin positions with equity, debt or preferred shares may find the price of capital changes when bond yields rise. NewsBTC recently examined how Strategy combined a Bitcoin purchase with preferred-share repurchases. Those funding mechanics deserve attention when monetary policy becomes less forgiving.

The market will now weigh Fed commentary, Treasury yields and subsequent employment and inflation releases. Traders should also distinguish a brief reaction to the 8:30 a.m. US data release from a sustained change in price direction; crypto trades continuously while other asset markets open and close on different clocks.

A higher-than-expected CPI reading is a real headwind for hopes of an imminent policy pivot. It is not a price target for Bitcoin. The stronger editorial conclusion is that the investment environment just became more demanding, particularly for leveraged traders who need both stable financing conditions and rising asset prices.

Arc was in second place, with $11.8 million. Ink added $4.2 million, and Tempo $2.7 million. After that, it fell off a cliff. OP Mainnet, zkSync, and Gnosis each grew by less than $200K, and Boba added under $20K.

Every chain from second to eighth together will get you roughly $19.2 million. And yet, Arbitrum’s number is more than three times that.

Here, it’s worth pointing out that this chart is a measure of growth. It is a measure of who added the most stablecoin trading in a week. Not who’s the biggest overall.

Normally, anyone with a block explorer can see all of that.

Now, whether it actually brings more users and volume is still an open question. However, it’s still reason enough to stick around.

ARB has had a decent run on the price charts lately.

After dipping to about $0.160 late on 8th November, ARB went up to $0.188 by next afternoon. It’s since pulled back a bit and at press time, ARB was trading near $0.1795.

That pointed to 12% above the dip, comfortably clear of the range it had been in for most of the near-term.

The RSI was close to 77 at the top; before it fell to to 58.2 later under its Signal Line at 64.1. The CMF is the one to watch though. It was barely below zero, after shortly going green during the rally. The price went up, but real buying pressure didn’t really follow.

The move has a better shot at sticking if ARB climbs above $0.172.

#5185. US Bitcoin ETFs Lose $201M as BlackRock Withdrawals Extend the Slump

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

US-listed spot Bitcoin funds recorded another difficult session on Monday, November 9. The updated Farside Investors flow table shows $201.0 million in net outflows across the group, extending a three-session losing streak. The important detail is that the negative total was not evenly spread across issuers: BlackRock’s IBIT recorded $213.2 million leaving, while Morgan Stanley’s MSBT attracted $22.1 million.

TL;DR: Spot Bitcoin ETFs lost a net $201.0 million on November 9, following $430.2 million on November 5 and $411.9 million on November 6. BlackRock drove Monday’s withdrawals, while one smaller fund reported inflows. The figures measure fund creations and redemptions, not the intentions of every Bitcoin investor.

The latest fully reported Farside figures put the consecutive negative sessions at roughly $1.04 billion in aggregate. Thursday’s $430.2 million loss was followed by Friday’s $411.9 million and then Monday’s $201.0 million. That progression shows persistent selling pressure, although Monday’s net outflow was smaller than either of the previous two sessions.

Our previous report on Friday’s fund activity documented a similar pattern of issuer concentration. Going back further, the November 2 breakdown shows why it is worth examining individual ETF lines rather than reading the aggregate as a single institutional vote.

On November 9, ARK Invest and 21Shares’ ARKB showed a $9.9 million outflow. The positive contribution from MSBT offset part of the selling elsewhere. Several other funds showed no daily net creation or redemption. That does not mean no shares changed hands on exchanges; secondary-market trading and flows into or out of a fund’s underlying Bitcoin exposure are different measures.

BlackRock’s fund is a large and closely watched route into Bitcoin for investors using traditional brokerage and portfolio systems. Its persistent redemptions can therefore affect the tone of market commentary disproportionately. But the identity of an ETF’s end investors is not published in a daily flow table, and a withdrawal cannot automatically be attributed to hedge funds, pensions or retail holders.

The distinction between net flows and assets under management matters as well. A fund’s reported assets can fall when Bitcoin’s price drops even without corresponding redemptions. Conversely, share creations can occur on a day when the asset price weakens. Analysts who blend these concepts can make demand appear stronger or weaker than it actually was.

The next test is whether the large-issuer selling eases over several completed reporting sessions. One positive day would interrupt the streak but would not, by itself, demonstrate that buyers have regained control. It is also worth watching whether inflows broaden across products or remain confined to a single issuer.

These numbers are current through the November 9 US trading session, not an estimate of November 10 while trading is still under way. A better measure of sentiment combines a completed ETF flow series with spot volume, derivatives positioning and the changing rates outlook. For now, the latest evidence is that listed-fund demand remains weak even as the scale of net redemptions has moderated from last week’s peaks.

#5184. XRP Ledger Testnet and Devnet Face Major November Reset Wiping All Data

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP ledger developers have been notified regarding upcoming resets for Testnet and Devnets. According to a statement, XRPL testnet and devnet are scheduled for a reset on November 25, 2026, to improve full history server stability.

Testnet and Devnet for XRP Ledger developers will be reset on Nov 25, 2026 at 5–6 PM PT. All on-chain state (accounts, tokens, AMMs, etc.) will be deleted and will need a re-fund and re-setup afterward. Other networks will continue to operate as usual, including Mainnet, Xahau, and the Hooks Testnet.

Ripple runs Testnet and Devnet, so the community can test out new features and integrations without risking real money.

The XRPL Testnet used to depend on a single full history server, which went down and cannot be recovered without a reset. To avoid this from happening again, an upgrade was performed to increase the server storage capacity.

The resets will delete all ledger data in Testnet and Devnet, including all accounts, transactions, balances, settings, offers, AMMs, escrows, and other data. Testnet will have its Amendment statuses restored to match Mainnet's. However, any AMMs that are re-created after the reset will generally have different account addresses. As a best practice, users are urged not to use the same addresses or key pairs on Mainnet and any test networks.

The important URLs, Network ID, and validator settings for both the devnet and testnets will stay the same. For XRPL users who run an xrpld server that is connected to testnet or Devnet, after the reset they should delete their database data and restart the server.

The last time the XRPL devnet saw a reset was on March 3, 2026. This was necessitated due to the Batch amendment, which required more development at the time and to prevent validators from becoming amendment-blocked, a Devnet reset was required.

The XRPL foundation has launched the XRP Ledger Education Month with up to $40,000 in awards for educators and content creators who explain upcoming amendments like the Lending Protocol or teach how the XRP Ledger works, the role of XRP and the core protocol philosophies.

Since XRPL 3.3.0, memory consumption has been reduced by up to 23% on nodes. Memory growth in XRPL nodes was observed in late summer 2025 and a fix shipped in xrpld 3.3.0.

#5183. Analysts project Bitcoin to move just 2.5% on today’s CPI data

The historical source estimated a CPI-window move, not a cash hedge premium or a directional forecast. Its volatility readings are not live quotes.

A pre-release Bitcoin options analysis estimated a 2.5% move for a window containing the November 10 US inflation report. Its comparison of past and expected volatility did not establish whether buying protection was cheap.

The analysis reports 3,183 BTC of long liquidations, approximately $245 million, on November 5 and another 2,450 BTC, approximately $181 million, on November 8. These are forced closures of bullish Bitcoin positions, rather than totals for the entire crypto market.

The study puts Bitcoin’s 30-day realized-volatility reading at 33.7 and Deribit’s DVOL index at 40, leaving a reported gap of 6.3 volatility points. These are the study’s readings, not live quotes. It does not document the realized measure’s annualization method, limiting how precisely it can be compared with annualized DVOL.

The observations concern the period before October’s Consumer Price Index release was scheduled for November 10 at 8:30 a.m. Eastern, or 13:30 UTC. They are not a post-release market update.

Realized volatility describes swings that have already happened. Deribit defines DVOL as a forward-looking, annualized measure covering the next 30 days. Its calculation draws on option prices at expiries on either side of that horizon and interpolates between them.

The two readings describe different stretches of time. A turbulent week can lift the backward-looking measure and bring it closer to implied volatility without demonstrating that the cash price of protection has fallen.

The Daily Digits’ November 10 CryptoQuant study compares measures that look in opposite directions.

The reported gap alone cannot establish whether a particular protective option is cheap.

No exact quote time is supplied. The diagram does not forecast future swings.

Thirty markers per side represent the reported trailing and forward 30-day horizons, not prices or exact quote endpoints. The source reports 33.7, 40.03 and a 6.33-point difference. Its realized-volatility annualization method is not documented, limiting comparability. No values are recalculated. The divider is undated because the study gives no exact quote timestamp.

No exact quote time is supplied. The diagram does not forecast future swings.

DVOL condenses option pricing into a single index reading. It does not give the cash premium for a particular protective option, so it cannot alone tell a Bitcoin holder what buying protection through CPI would cost.

The Daily Digits separately estimates a 2.5% Bitcoin move, approximately $1,884, for a window containing CPI. It reports forward implied volatility of 47.9% for that window versus 38.9% afterward. The study does not disclose the exact expiry endpoints or quote timestamp, so that estimate cannot serve as a current, reproducible protection quote.

That event-window estimate also differs from DVOL’s constant 30-day horizon. Combining the two would blur the distinction between pricing around one release and pricing a month of uncertainty.

Neither measure supplies a directional forecast. Deribit’s explanation allows for large Bitcoin moves upward as well as downward, and an implied move sets no boundary on the eventual outcome.

#5182. Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

Security measures within XRP’s native ledger ensure no holder on the rich-list gets to take over the chain.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse used the company’s Swell 2026 event to address a long-running claim that Ripple Labs effectively controls the XRP Ledger simply because it holds a large amount of XRP. He said ownership of the token does not confer authority over network rules or validation.

The XRP Ledger currently has more than 150 validators. Of the 35 on the current recommended unique node list, only one is operated by Ripple, roughly 2.9 percent.

Network operators decide which validators they trust and can configure their own lists rather than relying solely on any single published set.

Unlike proof-of-stake systems, XRP holdings do not grant extra voting weight. Independent validators reach agreement on transactions. Protocol amendments require at least 80 percent validator support for two consecutive weeks before they activate.

BOOOM! $XRP Holders🚨🚨🚨 @bgarlinghouse CEO of Ripple debunked one of the biggest misconceptions about XRP ledger!For everyone still claiming Ripple controls the XRP Ledger because it owns billions of XRP, listen to what Brad said at Ripple Swell 2026.Owning XRP does not… https://t.co/zyDgmkPmIM pic.twitter.com/mhpDrqLnXG

Ripple can propose software changes and invest in the ecosystem, but it cannot unilaterally force upgrades.

Three amendments have been activated this year: Permissioned Domains on February 4, Permissioned DEX on February 18, and Batch transactions on October 9, 2026.

Notably, these tools are designed to support more controlled environments for institutional applications while still operating on the shared ledger.

Several highly established financial firms have moved forward with XRP-related products. Aviva Investors launched a tokenized share class of its Sterling Liquidity Fund. Franklin Templeton, DBS Bank, and Ripple have outlined plans involving tokenized fund shares and RLUSD.

Standard Chartered has also announced live institutional custody of RLUSD on the ledger, with additional collateral work planned alongside Ripple and LMAX Group.

These bullish developments do not depend on Ripple controlling the ledger. They rely on the existing consensus rules, low transaction costs, and the ability of independent parties to issue assets and settle on the network.

Even without control of validation, XRP retains specific functions on the ledger. Transaction fees, currently as low as 0.00001 XRP, are destroyed. Funded accounts require a base reserve.

The OG token can also serve as an intermediary asset for bridging different issued currencies when that path offers better execution.

Mr. Garlinghouse’s comments restate a technical distinction that has been part of XRPL design for years: large XRP holdings do not equal governance power.

#5181. Ether ETFs Shed $184.6M in a Day as BlackRock Leads Redemptions

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

The latest numbers from US spot Ether exchange-traded funds are another reminder that crypto’s institutional wrappers do not always move in sync with the networks beneath them. Farside Investors reports $184.6 million of net withdrawals on Monday, November 9. BlackRock’s ETHA accounted for $119.3 million, making it the largest identifiable contributor to the day’s negative reading.

TL;DR: US spot Ether ETFs lost $184.6 million on November 9. BlackRock’s ETHA recorded $119.3 million in outflows, with additional withdrawals from several other products. The latest session extends a run of weakness, but ETF flows alone cannot tell investors whether Ethereum’s network use or long-term adoption is improving.

Monday’s fund table is more revealing than a single headline number. Besides ETHA, the negative entries included Grayscale’s ETH product at $25 million, BlackRock’s ETHB at $20.8 million, Fidelity’s FETH at $19.1 million and TETH at $2 million. Morgan Stanley’s MSSE reported a smaller $1.6 million positive flow. Netting these issuer-level movements produces Farside’s total of $184.6 million leaving the category.

It was the third consecutive negative session, after withdrawals of $99.9 million on November 5 and $93.3 million on November 6. Across those dates, approximately $377.8 million left the tracked funds on a net basis. NewsBTC’s earlier look at the November 5 outflow provides a useful baseline, while our October divergence report shows how quickly relative Bitcoin and Ether fund demand can change.

An ETF owns or obtains exposure to ETH for its shareholders. It does not capture every place ETH is held, used as collateral, staked or exchanged. The Ethereum network also supports stablecoin transfers, decentralized finance applications, tokenized securities and settlement activity. Those services can grow or contract independently of whether a US investment wrapper receives subscriptions on a particular day.

Equally, network activity is not automatically a bullish signal for ETH’s market price. Fees, token issuance, layer-two activity and competition for users influence how economic use may translate into demand for the asset. Sensible analysis keeps both sets of evidence in view rather than choosing whichever currently supports a favored market narrative.

Bitcoin funds also recorded negative flows on November 9. That indicates this is not simply a one-day rotation from ETH into BTC within the US spot ETF category. Still, the products differ in investor base, structure, age and exposure, so the two daily totals should not be converted into a precise conclusion about relative institutional conviction.

One additional complication is reporting timing. Daily ETF tables are finalized after the US market closes and may be revised when an issuer reports late. Quoting an unfinished session as a completed result could create a false impression of reversal or acceleration. The figures here are dated specifically to November 9, and that time stamp should remain with the headline wherever the article is shared.

The next completed sessions will show whether ETHA’s withdrawals persist and whether other products begin drawing offsetting demand. Until then, Monday’s figures are strong evidence of weak investment-fund flows—not a definitive reading of the wider Ethereum economy or a forecast of the token’s next move.

#5180. Bitcoin – How BTC could turn its recent price crash into an $87K rally

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Is Bitcoin’s [BTC] growing negative correlation to other risk assets becoming a key headwind?

It has been less than 48 hours since the most recent crypto wipeout resulted in over $1 billion in liquidations, pushing Bitcoin close to $70k, and sending market sentiment into extreme fear. While Bitcoin continues to trade below $75k, the S&P500 has risen by over 2%, pointing to stronger resilience despite the ongoing FUD.

This growing disparity raises questions about Bitcoin’s performance as a macro hedge this cycle. Notably, Peter Schiff shares his view. As his post below highlights, the long-time Bitcoin critic has warned of a deeper BTC correction, citing its underperformance compared to other risk assets. Even precious metals such as gold and silver have gained over 2%, further widening the performance gap.

Against this backdrop, positioning for further downside is starting to make more sense.

According to CoinGlass data, Bitcoin’s long/short ratio is showing bearish signs as it fell back from a 3-month high of 1.4 on the 7th of November, indicating traders are returning to shorting their positions, signaling weaker confidence in BTC’s near-term recovery. The key takeaway? There is no downside liquidity left to be hunted. The recent $1 billion liquidation event has wiped out almost all long-side liquidity.

This can be considered a great chance for bulls to recover. If Bitcoin remains above $75k and accumulation continues, then both early and late short positions could come under pressure. In fact, one analyst highlighted over $11 billion in short liquidity clustered around $87k.

Interestingly, bulls already seem to be positioning for this potential move.

Despite the scale of the recent wipeout, market sentiment is already showing signs of recovery.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index shows that sentiment has improved by four points to 32. This indicates that selling pressure may be losing steam and that confidence is returning. Notably, institutional activity is also adding to this shift. As on-chain data reported, MSTR recently bought an additional 1,036 BTC.

To add to this, MARA Holdings has announced a Q3 net profit of $634.4 million, up from $123.1 million in the previous year. This is backed up by Santiment data showing a distinct increase in accumulation between retail and mid-sized holders. In the past two weeks the market has seen an increase of 1-10 BTC wallets by 10,076 BTC and 10-100 BTC wallets by 4,131 BTC.

In short, just because BTC has failed to outperform other assets does not mean that the rally is dying.

Despite macro uncertainty and bearish positioning being a key factor, there are clear signs of increasing institutional exposure and accumulation across wallet classes. If Bitcoin can hold above $75k, the current divergence could turn into a potential bear trap, with the $87k short-liquidity cluster becoming the next major upside target.

Bitcoin is lagging traditional markets, but institutional buying and whale accumulation remain strong.

If Bitcoin holds $75k, a short squeeze could push prices toward $87k.

#5179. XRP Ledger Introduces Quantum-Proof Security Upgrade to Users

As the XRP Ledger developers continue to make committed efforts to make the network fully quantum-resistant in the coming year, the team has just proposed a major upgrade that has sparked a buzz across the XRP community.

Sparking fresh excitement across the XRP community earlier today, the XRP Ledger (XRPL) Foundation has disclosed plans to introduce quantum-resistant accounts to users.

The team revealed plans to introduce a new feature to the XRP Ledger to offer users strict protection against future quantum computing threats without paying additional transaction fees.

While it is yet to be implemented, the proposed design will allow users to add a NIST-approved ML-DSA-44 signing key to their accounts and disable their existing keys.

This way, it will become harder for potential quantum attacks to compromise accounts, especially those whose public keys have already been exposed.

Apart from the extra layer of protection the upgrade promises to offer users, its cost-effective design has stirred more interest among the XRP community.

Notably, users will not have to pay higher transaction fees to access the additional security, as the proposed feature allows XRP holders to strengthen their account protection without worrying about extra transaction costs.

The proposed upgrade sends relief across the crypto ecosystem as it addresses concerns about the possibility of increasingly powerful quantum computers eventually breaking some of the cryptographic systems currently used to secure blockchains and cryptocurrencies.

While the upgrade is yet to launch, the team revealed that it has scheduled a migration study for the first half of 2027.

The study is aimed at examining how existing accounts can transition to the new security system and what the process would involve for users.

The proposed security upgrade has further sparked more optimism as it is also part of Ripple's broader aim of achieving full quantum protection by 2028.

#5178. MARA Sells 13,597 Bitcoin for $1.1B as Its AI Pivot Reshapes Treasury Strategy

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

MARA has put a sizeable number on the changing economics of Bitcoin mining. In its third-quarter shareholder letter, the company said it sold 13,597 BTC after September 30 for approximately $1.1 billion, at an average selling price of about $81,039. It used $750 million of the proceeds to repay Bitcoin-collateralized loans, releasing 17,477 BTC that had been pledged as security.

TL;DR: MARA disclosed $1.1 billion of post-quarter Bitcoin sales and repayment of $750 million in BTC-backed borrowing. The miner is increasingly monetizing its coin reserves to support AI infrastructure and other capital priorities. This marks a meaningful departure from its earlier strategy of holding mined Bitcoin.

At the end of September, MARA reported 34,012 BTC in total holdings, including a sizeable portion lent out or pledged as collateral. The newly disclosed disposals followed quarter-end, so that September balance should not be presented as the company’s current holding without adjustment. MARA said the repayments reduced the proportion of Bitcoin lent or pledged to less than 5% of its remaining holdings, versus roughly 57% at the earlier reporting date.

Management describes the move as a way to gain financial flexibility while still using Bitcoin strategically. Borrowing against the coins had helped finance the proposed acquisition of the Long Ridge natural-gas power business. The subsequent sale allowed MARA to clear the borrowing before the transaction closed, instead of leaving its financing exposed to future collateral calls if Bitcoin weakened.

That is a trade-off, not a free improvement. Selling reserve assets reduces direct exposure to any subsequent Bitcoin recovery. Keeping more BTC would preserve that upside but could require accepting additional liquidity and borrowing risk. The appropriate decision depends on the company’s priorities and its ability to earn a return on the infrastructure being funded.

MARA’s third-quarter revenue fell about 35% from a year earlier to $164.2 million. Its mining operations still produce coins, but the company is now emphasizing power assets and potential data-center customers alongside hashrate. Long Ridge includes generating capacity and land that could support a data-center campus. Development plans are not the same as signed, revenue-producing leases.

The move also fits a wider sector pattern. NewsBTC recently reported how Riot sold more than 20,000 BTC while pursuing AI-related data-center agreements. The contrast with Strive’s rapid Bitcoin accumulation is useful: public companies can use the same underlying asset in radically different corporate strategies.

MARA also disclosed authorization for up to $500 million in share repurchases, while its reported quarterly profit included substantial accounting effects linked to digital assets. Those items should not be mistaken for recurring mining cash flow. Nor does an authorization guarantee that every dollar of the program will be spent.

The follow-up questions are whether Long Ridge closes, whether planned data-center leases are signed, how much Bitcoin remains unrestricted, and whether new AI revenue eventually justifies the capital committed. The BTC sale is confirmed. The return on the assets it may finance is still an execution story.

#5177. Any Bitcoin recovery faces a cash problem as Binance’s stablecoins fall to $32 billion

In one November 10 sample around 10:42 UTC, Binance had a greater value of Bitcoin buy orders than OKX within 0.05% below each venue’s midpoint. The $32 billion reserve figure covers ERC-20 stablecoins only.

Binance's tracked ERC-20 stablecoin reserves stand near $32 billion after a substantial contraction, giving traders a reason to test the exchange's remaining buying capacity against actual Bitcoin orders. A November 10 comparison with OKX showed Binance had a higher total value of nearby Bitcoin buy orders in one narrow slice of the market, even as its tracked stablecoin balance remained below October levels.

Community analyst Novaque Research via CryptoQuant put the balance at roughly $32 billion on Nov. 10, down from about $41 billion to $42 billion in late October. Its reserve comparison also put OKX near $9 billion, with balances trending lower there. That offers little support for a simple reading that Binance's lost tracked reserves have reappeared at that rival.

Bitcoin was trading around $74,000 on Nov. 10. Coinbase's BTC-USD ticker recorded a last trade of $74,056 at 10:41:58 UTC. The reserve observations arrive after the Oct. 29 hack at Binance and subsequent reopening of withdrawals, which CryptoSlate covered last week.

In a separate Nov. 9 post, community analyst oinonen_t via CryptoQuant reported a 25.6% decline in Binance's ERC-20 stablecoin reserves from an early-October starting point to 32 billion units. The same analysis reported a smaller 7.6% drop in Bitcoin reserves to approximately 673,000 BTC.

Those figures suggest a larger proportional retreat in the tracked stablecoin balance. However, they use an early-October comparison, while Novaque's latest post refers to late October. The exact synchronized dates and wallet coverage are unspecified, so combining them would overstate what is known about the effect of the hack.

There is also a network boundary. ERC-20 balances exclude stablecoins held on other networks. Novaque cautions that wallet restructuring and cross-chain transfers may explain part of the contraction. CryptoSlate's earlier reporting on Binance's Tether routing likewise showed why a decline in identified Ethereum balances does not establish an equivalent withdrawal of customer dollars.

CryptoQuant defines exchange reserves as coins held in exchange addresses and describes stablecoin reserves as potential buying power. Potential matters here: an inventory figure does not say how much is being offered to buy Bitcoin at a particular price.

Around 10:42 UTC on Nov. 10, a snapshot of Binance's public BTC-USDT book showed approximately 1,980,000 USDT of displayed buy orders within 5 basis points, or 0.05%, below its midpoint. The near-contemporaneous OKX book showed about 980,000 USDT within the same width below its own midpoint.

The midpoint is halfway between the highest buy price and lowest sell price. Summing each eligible buy order's price multiplied by its Bitcoin quantity gives the quoted USDT value. Both returned books extended beyond the measured band, allowing a complete comparison at that width. The amounts are in USDT, distinct from Coinbase's US-dollar trade.

Binance therefore displayed more buying interest at those nearby prices in this sample. But orders can change before execution, and one pair of observations cannot establish enduring depth, deterioration from an earlier period or a return to pre-hack conditions. It measures what was displayed then, rather than how much Bitcoin buyers actually purchased.

Customer backing answers another question. Binance says its proof of reserves concerns assets held in custody for users. That measure does not identify which balances customers have committed to Bitcoin buy orders. Customer inventory, Binance's corporate resources and posted buying orders cannot be treated as one interchangeable pool.

The order-book snapshot preceded the scheduled US inflation release. The Bureau of Labor Statistics calendar listed October CPI for Nov. 10 at 8:30 a.m. Eastern, or 13:30 UTC.

For holders assessing a rebound, stronger executed spot buying alongside buy orders that are replaced as sellers execute would challenge the concern that the smaller tracked stablecoin balance means impaired trading liquidity. Continued reserve contraction accompanied by persistently thinner depth at comparable widths would strengthen that concern.

#5176. Ethereum – Will ETH’s $432M liquidation shock put $2K support at risk?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 8th and 9th of November, Ethereum [ETH] witnessed a combined liquidation of $432.34 million worth of leveraged positions. As a result, the ETH price slid from $2,236 to $2,060.

This was the largest wave of liquidations for Ethereum since the 19th of August, when $1.02 billion worth of short positions were taken out in a single day.

At the time of writing, Ethereum was trading just above $2.1k. Swing traders and investors would be keenly watching the $2,000 psychological level to assess if the next wave of selling was upon us.

In other news, Vitalik Buterin presented reasons why Ethereum as a store of value made “more sense to focus on” than ETH as a fee-burning token.

The 30-day rolling Ethereum Open Interest Z-Score fell to -2.87. Using data from Binance, crypto analyst Arab Chain presented data that revealed an unusually sharp decline in Open Interest (OI) behind ETH contracts.

The OI was at $5.236 billion, compared to the 30-day average of $5.850 billion, with a standard deviation of $213.77 million. This meant that the current OI was about 2.87 standard deviations below the 30-day mean.

Meanwhile, persistent selling pressure has pushed the leading altcoin’s price down towards $2,100. Declining OI and downward price pressure could be due to the closure of existing positions, through liquidations or manual exits.

It is not necessarily a sign that there were sizable short positions opened, the analyst wrote.

The ETH price structure was bearish after the loss of the swing point at $2,358. The $2,400 area has since been retested as a resistance zone, and the price has reacted bearishly from there in November.

The OBV was relatively flat over the past ten days, but it has been declining slowly since early October. The MACD was also declining and signaled strong bearish momentum at the time of writing.

The $2,000 round-number support was one level to watch. The $1,840-$1,950 area demand zone where ETH consolidated in July and August was also a place investors can wait for a bullish reaction from.

Upbit remains one of the largest holders of Dogecoin and Shiba Inu on the market, being the key place where pressure on the prices of those two assets arises. According to Arkham, the South Korean exchange holds $816.81 million worth of DOGE and $284.75 million worth of SHIB, which is around $1.1 billion combined. In coins, that's 11.766 billion DOGE and a whopping 64.134 trillion SHIB. Upbit's Dogecoin balance has been growing for years, and the current reading is essentially the highest on Arkham's chart.

Shiba Inu's numbers are even more interesting. Total SHIB exchange reserves currently sit at around 86 trillion tokens, so Upbit most likely holds about three quarters of all SHIB available on exchanges, if both data sources track the same wallets.

Shiba Inu has been struggling on the market for ages; however, there is finally an exchange outflow of approximately 144 billion SHIB in the last 24 hours. It's a drop in the ocean though: exchange reserves went down by only 0.17%, and around 183 billion SHIB still flowed into exchanges over the same period.

The current price level is the lowest point on the chart in the last year.

SHIB is in the same position, trading at $0.00000443 after breaking below the ascending trendline that supported it since July. The closest support is the July low around $0.0000041, with the moving averages above it aiming downwards.

RSI for both assets is moving around 35, so the market isn't oversold yet. With this much DOGE and SHIB sitting on one exchange, selling from Upbit users could add even more pressure on the price, unfortunately.

#5174. Strategy Adds 1,036 BTC While Repurchasing $188M of STRC Preferred Shares

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Strategy’s newest capital-allocation update contains two transactions that point in different directions. In a November 9 filing announcement, the company said it acquired 1,036 additional Bitcoin and bought back approximately $188 million of STRC preferred shares. The first adds to exposure to BTC; the second reduces outstanding securities in its wider financing structure.

TL;DR: Strategy has disclosed a 1,036-BTC purchase alongside $188 million of STRC repurchases. The combination shows that the company’s Bitcoin strategy involves ongoing capital management, not simply accumulation. These are separate transactions with different effects on liquidity, risk and future obligations.

Strategy is closely associated with long-term Bitcoin accumulation, and for good reason: its treasury is far larger than that of most public companies. Yet the recurring filings increasingly include details about preferred securities, reserves and capital raising that are central to understanding how that position is financed.

A Bitcoin purchase turns available funds into an asset whose market value can change around the clock. Repurchasing preferred shares does something else. It reduces the quantity of a security issued to outside investors and may alter future distribution obligations, depending on that security’s terms. One action raises direct BTC exposure; the other changes the claims that investors have on Strategy’s capital structure.

That distinction was visible in an earlier update covered by NewsBTC, when Strategy bought 811 BTC and repurchased $208.2 million of STRC. The new announcement continues that paired approach, rather than revealing a sudden switch away from its central investment thesis.

Preferred securities can offer investors contractual distributions or other rights that differ from ordinary equity. Their terms, market prices and the company’s reserve position matter for both existing holders and common shareholders. Buying them back may be attractive when they trade at prices the issuer considers advantageous, but it also uses liquidity that could otherwise remain in reserve or fund more purchases.

There is a useful comparison with other Bitcoin treasury companies, which have their own funding methods and varying tolerance for leverage. The phrase ‘corporate Bitcoin buyer’ hides important differences in debt maturities, preferred distributions, equity issuance and the sources of cash used to acquire coins.

Strategy’s public metrics indicate Bitcoin holdings around 850,364 BTC following the update. Readers should use the company’s dated disclosure when comparing holdings and purchase prices rather than combining a fresh transaction with an older snapshot. Market-value figures can change between the filing and the moment a reader opens the article.

Another consideration is the difference between a repurchase authorization and completed repurchases. Here the company is describing actual STRC repurchase activity, which should be counted separately from any standing authority to buy securities later. The price paid and reduction in outstanding shares determine its economic impact; simply repeating the dollar amount cannot answer those questions.

None of this proves that the company’s securities or Bitcoin will rise. A more useful test is whether Strategy can keep servicing its obligations while making purchases in a period of changing interest rates and volatile digital-asset prices. The November 9 announcement confirms it is still doing both jobs—adding coins and adjusting the financing architecture that supports the strategy.

#5173. Democratic Sweep Puts Crypto Bill On a Cleaner Track

Home » Regulations » Democratic Sweep Puts Crypto Bill On a Cleaner Track

Regaining the majority at the Senate has shuffled the cards for crypto legislation: will Democrats handle it better?

Democrats’ control of both chambers of Congress has raised fresh questions about how soon Washington can deliver a lasting framework for the digital asset industry.

The stalled CLARITY Act and related market-structure legislation now sit before a Congress that is expected to reopen the terms, priorities, and timing of any overhaul.

The CLARITY Act was meant to settle a core industry question: which federal regulator oversees different parts of the crypto market. Its path was already uncertain.

The new congressional balance adds further negotiation over consumer protections, disclosure rules, clearer exchange oversight, and the split of authority between agencies.

Democrats Want to Rewrite the CLARITY Act and Crypto Could Pay the PriceDemocrats are reportedly preparing a major rewrite of the CLARITY Act after their midterm victory.The proposed changes could give the SEC greater oversight of crypto markets. The language, highlighted by… pic.twitter.com/DepyDC7V2J

Democratic majorities are likely to place a crystal-clear emphasis on investor safeguards and enforcement tools before endorsing a broad framework favored by many digital-asset firms.

Quick legislation is not ruled out, but any significant bill that advances could look different and move more slowly than earlier expectations.

Recently, Federal agencies have continued examining registration pathways and rules for crypto trading platforms even as Congress deliberates.

Crypto firms therefore face an evolving patchwork of agency actions while lawmakers work toward a more permanent statutory settlement.

Industry legal observers now see comprehensive market-structure rules crucial for the CLARITY Act potentially slipping into 2027.

Bitcoin’s recent rebound and renewed inflows into spot Bitcoin ETFs show that institutional interest has held up despite the political uncertainty.

Those ETF flows have offered the market some support, yet they do not resolve the regulatory questions still facing exchanges, token issuers, and decentralized-finance projects.

Market participants can already gain exposure through regulated products while much of the underlying industry waits for clearer operating rules.

A longer legislative process would likely preserve that divide, favoring firms able to absorb compliance costs and adapt to agency guidance.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

If the trend does not reverse itself, the altcoin market could be heading into another capitulation phase.

Looking at this from a technical point of view, TOTAL2 is just 15% away from erasing all of its Q3 profits. This could put short-term holders in the red and continue to cause capital outflows. This is getting increasingly risky as Ethereum’s Fear and Greed Index has already fallen by nearly 60% in Q4. In short, the overall sentiment in the altcoin space is now looking weaker.

Against this backdrop, Charles Schwab’s latest announcement has given the altcoin sector a boost. The $13 trillion TradFi giant took to X to announce it has added Solana, Avalanche, and Chainlink to its crypto trading accounts, allowing eligible retail clients to buy and sell all three tokens directly.

Naturally, the bigger question is what this means for DeFi-TradFi integration.

The key takeaway? The timing of Schwab’s move could hardly be better.

However, trading volumes may be starting to normalize. In September, altcoin volume reached nearly 4x Bitcoin’s, hinting at heavy speculation. The latest cooldown could point to a healthier market structure.

In this context, Charles Schwab’s move could be more than just another step in TradFi-DeFi integration.

Instead, Schwab’s addition could create a new avenue for retail liquidity. Whilst it may not result in an instant revival, it will make SOL, AVAX and LINK more accessible, increasing retail involvement and helping revive a September-style wave of altcoin adoption.

Altcoins face more selling pressure as market sentiment and liquidity weaken.

#5171. Strategy spent more than twice as much on preferred buybacks as Bitcoin last week

The November 9 filing shows common-stock proceeds funding Bitcoin purchases and a larger STRC buyback during November 2-8.

Bitcoin treasury company Strategy funded its Bitcoin purchases and a larger STRC preferred-stock buyback with common-stock sales during Nov. 2-8, while recording no sales through its four preferred-stock at-the-market programs, according to its Nov. 9 filing.

The company raised $284 million in net proceeds by selling 2,051,348 MSTR common shares. It directed $188 million to STRC repurchases, $82 million to Bitcoin and $14 million to its USD Cash balance.

Common shareholders financed both more Bitcoin and fewer preferred shares outstanding. The repurchases can reduce future preferred-dividend requirements, while issuing MSTR increases the number of common shares. STRC's prospective return as a funding source had yet to produce disclosed sales during that week.

During Nov. 2-8, 2026, common-stock proceeds funded a larger STRC buyback than Bitcoin purchase.

STRC repurchases absorbed 66.3% of MSTR net proceeds; Bitcoin purchases used 28.9%.

CryptoSlate's Nov. 5 analysis described a possible reopening of STRC funding following its price recovery.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

An improved trading price and a completed capital raise are separate events. In its July 27 policy announcement, Strategy said it would not issue STRC below $100 per share and expected repurchases to taper as the security approached that level. Those policies were statements of intent that could change.

A price recovery can improve the conditions for selling new preferred shares. Cash reaches Strategy when it actually issues them. The latest filing records no STRC sales for Nov. 2-8, nor any sales in the STRF, STRK or STRD ATM rows.

Strategy acquired 1,036 BTC for $82 million during the week, at an average $79,012 per coin including fees and expenses.

The larger use of that capital was the repurchase of 1,887,464 STRC shares for $188 million.

Strategy's issuer briefing explains that common equity sits behind debt and preferred stock, so removing preferred claims can strengthen the residual economics available to common shareholders.

Funding those purchases by issuing common stock increases the share count. Existing holders therefore share the remaining economics with more shares outstanding. Whether the combined transaction improves value per share depends on the issuance terms, repurchase price and use of proceeds.

This extends the capital-allocation tension described in CryptoSlate's Nov. 2 coverage of deployable cash pressure.

As of Nov. 8, Strategy held $4.77 billion in its USD Reserve and $146 million in USD Cash. The reserve is intended to support preferred-stock dividends and interest on outstanding debt. USD Cash serves broader treasury purposes, including buying Bitcoin, expanding the reserve and capital management.

Strategy used $21 million of the reserve to pay preferred dividends during Nov. 2-8. The $14 million allocated from MSTR proceeds went to USD Cash.

@saylor's Nov. 9 post summarized the company's position as 850,364 BTC and about $4.9 billion in USD assets. That broad dollar figure includes balances with distinct designated purposes; the filing separately identifies the cash available for broader treasury deployment.

#5170. Binance Shuts Down Transfers Involving MEXC and Its Affiliates

Just a few days after the U.S. Treasury took action to cut global crypto exchange MEXC off from the U.S. financial system, Binance has also announced strict regulatory actions against the exchange.

On Tuesday, November 10, Binance, the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange by volume, announced its immediate decision to halt all transfers involving MEXC and all its affiliated companies.

Binance released an official announcement earlier today, warning users that it has stopped all trading services related to MEXC following new concerns about its legitimacy.

While the restrictions were announced to take effect immediately, Binance explained that its move follows the recent decision by the U.S. Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) to designate MEXC as a Primary Money Laundering Concern.

As such, Binance has prohibited its users from directly or indirectly sending funds to, receiving funds from, or conducting transactions through Binance involving MEXC and its related entities.

The MEXC-affiliated entities mentioned by Binance include MEXC Global Limited, MEXC Estonia OÜ, MEXC Singapore Pte Ltd, MEXC Global Foundation Ltd, GIDA Technology AG, and several other companies and affiliates operating under the MEXC name.

Although MEXC is among the top 20 crypto exchanges in the world, FinCEN issued an order against MEXC on November 6, cutting the exchange off from the U.S. financial system amid recent fraud allegations.

Notably, the U.S. Treasury accused the exchange of facilitating at least $16 billion in illicit transactions tied to sanctions evasion and cybercrime and of defrauding people of their funds.

While Binance remains the first crypto exchange to take action in accordance with FinCEN’s rule, it appears that many other exchanges are lining up to take similar action.

#5169. Visa Adds Monad to Stablecoin Settlement Options as Institutions Seek More Rails

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Visa’s growing list of blockchain settlement options reportedly has a new entrant: Monad. According to a November 10 report citing the Monad Foundation, participating Visa issuers and acquirers can now use the network for stablecoin settlement. That is a back-office financial infrastructure development, rather than a new consumer-facing Visa card that automatically makes every merchant accept cryptocurrency.

When a cardholder pays at a shop, the retail transaction and the subsequent movement of funds between financial institutions are separate processes. A settlement service can use digital dollars on a blockchain to transfer value between participating institutions without requiring consumers to interact with a crypto wallet. That is the layer Visa has been expanding.

Visa’s October 15 update on stablecoin-powered money movement showed that parts of its broader digital-dollar strategy were moving from pilots toward live commercial deployment. Monad’s reported addition extends the selection of networks available to eligible partners; it should not be confused with a universal launch in every market or for every Visa transaction.

For an issuer or acquirer, more than one usable settlement rail can provide choices around transaction processing, cost, operational resilience and supported assets. But multiple chains also bring added technical and compliance responsibilities. Connecting a network is the beginning of integration, not proof that it will process meaningful volumes immediately.

NewsBTC previously examined how Visa’s treasury engine targets institutional settlement. Separately, Reap’s Monad-linked card infrastructure plan illustrates a different use of the same chain: backing consumer card activity, not necessarily settling balances between institutions.

A blockchain being listed among supported options can improve its credibility with enterprise developers, but that does not establish a direct relationship between network adoption and the market price of its native token. Fees, throughput, partner concentration and the way transaction costs are paid all influence the economics.

What matters next is confirmation of which issuers and acquirers are live, which stablecoins and regions are supported, how settlement is reconciled when something fails, and what measurable transaction volume arrives. Those details would turn a promising infrastructure announcement into evidence of commercial activity. Until then, this is a meaningful new option—not an automatic overhaul of how Visa payments work everywhere.

#5168. Shiba Inu (SHIB) Scammers Fake 'Ryoshi' to Push 137 Scam Tokens and Dupe Investors

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to longtime SHIB community member Mazrael, since the SHIB deployer wallet went silent in October 2021, at least 137 scam tokens have falsely claimed that the SHIB deployer sent them the coins. The intent of scammers is to 'fake' Shiba Inu's pseudonymous founder, "Ryoshi," and the reason behind this is to appear legitimate: If "Ryoshi" sent it, people trust it.

Mazrael explains how this trick works: a token is just a program, and it keeps its own record of transfers. The scammer can write any address into that record as the sender. So these scammers write the SHIB deployer's address, and on Etherscan it looks like "Ryoshi" sent the token. However, the real SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021.

How scammers fake "Ryoshi" Since the SHIB deployer wallet went silent in October 2021, at least 137 scam tokens have pretended it sent them their coins. Why? To look legitimate. If "Ryoshi" sent it, people trust it. How the trick works: A token is just a program, and it keeps its own record of transfers. The scammer can write ANY address into that record as the sender. So they write the SHIB deployer's address, and on Etherscan it looks like "Ryoshi" sent you their token. But the deployer never signed anything. Click the transaction itself and look at "From". That's the wallet that really sent it, and it's never the SHIB deployer. The numbers: - 9,131+ fake transfers - 2,500+ wallets hit - 0 signed by the deployer Some of the fakes: APE, three "SHIBA INU2.0" tokens, ShibChain, SHIB DAO, METARUFFY, a token literally called "Ryoshi", Caw and Baby Caw, three fake "Shibarium" tokens (a year before the real one), "BONE2.0" and three "UNLEASHED DAO" tokens. Many got shilled by OGs and ex-admins as "Ryoshi-backed" to make them look legit. The real SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021. Always check who signed the transaction, not what the token says.

'The SHIB deployer never signed anything,' Mazrael stated, urging the crypto community to click on related token transactions and examine the "From" address, saying this is the wallet that sent it and was never the SHIB deployer.

Mazrael shared the numbers, outlining over 9,131 fake transfers, over 2,500 wallets hit, and zero signed by the SHIB deployer. He went on to name some of these fake tokens, adding that some of these tokens got promoted as "Ryoshi-backed" to make them look legitimate.

This disclosure warns crypto users to always check who signed the transaction, not what the token says.

In a separate post, Mazrael explained that nothing links the SHIB deployer address to any person.

The SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) only deployed one contract: SHIB, and it hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021. It has no public signed message on record, either on-chain or on Etherscan.

LEASH, BONE, and TREAT each came from different deployers. Mazrael indicated that BONE's deployer is shiba-swap.eth, whereas LEASH came from '0xA221…1494,' the same key that deployed StarLink, whose own site names Woof Decentra as LEASH's developer. That key kept LEASH's rebase oracle, an admin key, for five years.

#5167. XRP’s Quantum-Resistant Design Comes Into Focus

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP’s Quantum-Resistant Design Comes Into Focus

The foundation behind XRP Ledger aims to mitigate quantum resistance hazards ahead of the practical test.

The chain developers behind Ripple’s native XRP Ledger are actively preparing to implement quantum-resistant measures. For this reason, the devs picked ML-DSA-44, a digital signature standard that the National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST) approved in 2024.

Earlier this year, Ripple introduced a multi-phase roadmap to prepare the XRP Ledger (XRPL) for a post-quantum future, with a target for full readiness by 2028. The new signature format needs 3,732 bytes for public keys and signatures, raising transaction data requirements. This will be audited in mid 2027.

JUST IN: $XRP Ledger unveils quantum-resistant account plan, allowing users to upgrade to quantum-proof security with no additional transaction fees as Ripple targets full quantum protection by 2028. pic.twitter.com/wS2Mk46nG8

As described in Google’s quantum computing research paper, there’s a few key risks the crypto sphere has to handle. The most quoted one is the exposable public key risks when users transact within the blockchain, something that quantum-computing based hackers could take serious advantage of.

There’s a more subtle risk too, one elliptic curve researchers call “harvest now, decrypt later.” Simply put, bad actors can collect publicly visible cryptographic data from the blockchain today and hold onto it. This raises the chances of the account eventually being broken into.

🚨 MASSIVE: THE $XRP LEDGER IS PREPARING FOR QUANTUM COMPUTERS. HERE IS WHAT NOBODY IS TELLING YOU.Every payment you sign reveals your public key.A powerful enough quantum computer could work backward from it.Any account that has ever sent a payment is already exposed.The… https://t.co/hpCLy5Gzzi pic.twitter.com/L32SemqQcE

The extra XRP network storage needed for this kind of upgrade doesn’t necessarily raise the fees. Node operators are absorbing the ~3.6 KB increase per protected transaction (from 105 bytes to 3,732 bytes for key plus the signature), as documented in the proposal. With database overhead, every secure payment would use 5,331 bytes of storage.

David Schwartz Explains What Could Really Drive XRP's ValueFormer Ripple (@Ripple) CTO David Schwartz (@JoelKatz) says using $XRP to pay XRPL (@XRP) gas fees is unlikely to drive its price significantly.He argues that broader network adoption could create value for XRP… pic.twitter.com/X0et9M7El9

The XRP Ledger foundation is planning the inaugural phase of the quantum resistance study in early 2027 as devs polish the proposition of key generation between the Python, JavaScript and server versions. Mitigations are done within a zero-knowledge proof to validate results without disclosing vectors.

Quantum physics may be a tough nut to crack for most crypto currencies, but XRP’s developers are set on a mission to get the chain shielded by 2028.

#5166. Is Ethereum’s slide below $2250 sign of ‘impatience of markets’?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Ethereum has lost support at $2,250 recently, last seen in the month of October, as the altcoin was trading at $2,126.73 at press time.

In fact, in just one month, ETH saw a drop of 15.1%. This drop and the struggle to climb to the $2500 price level have got everyone talking in the market.

Starting with a known crypto analyst under the username TED, who said,

$ETH lost its $2,250 support zone and quickly dropped below $2,100. Now, Ethereum needs to reclaim that zone soon, or else it could drop below $2,000.

Echoing similar sentiments, another trader added,

The immediate support for $ETH aligns with the $2,000 psychological threshold, reinforced by the May 28 high around $1,967.

However, the founder of NowcastIQ, Andreas Steno Larsen, believes that it’s not just ETH but a broader market downturn, as he noted,

Prices are in free fall into November on everything from shipping to insurance to crack spreads, so seems like November will deliver potentially the softest inflation report on this side of Covid.

Bitmine’s Tom Lee responded to ETH’s current downturn and added,

To me, the languishing of $ETH price, while disappointing is arguably more a reflection of the impatience of markets

Lee believes that investors are becoming impatient for immediate returns, even as Ethereum [ETH] continues to make steady progress in the tokenization of real-world assets (RWAs).

According to Token Terminal data, Ethereum RWA’s market capitalization has reached $20.9 billion, followed by BNB Chain at $6.2 billion and Solana at $4.8 billion.

He even quoted Vitalik Buterin’s recent view of ETH as a store of value, which further strengthens its long-term investment narrative.

However, Lee’s optimistic outlook on ETH is not fitting well into the market, as Ethereum has recorded a weak performance in November 2026, declining 5.50% so far.

CoinGlass data showed ETH had fallen 5.50% in November, 20.67% in Q4 and 28.25% year to date.

The Liquidation Heatmap further shows that $269.37 million in crypto positions were liquidated over the past 24 hours. Of this, Ethereum recorded the highest coin-specific liquidations at $63.76 million, followed by Bitcoin [BTC] at $61.42 million at press time.

At the same time, an Ethereum OG whale has moved 2,155 ETH worth approximately $4.59 million after more than 10 years of inactivity.

Moreover, US spot Ethereum ETFs have been seeing weekly withdrawals since the week ending the 9th of October to date.

Still, there is hope in the market that there could be trend reversals, as whale activity around Lido’s staked Ethereum (stETH) has surged over the past four days, reaching its highest sustained level since April, with more than 630 daily transactions worth at least $100,000.

This suggests that large investors are actively repositioning around ETH near $2,100.

#5165. Slower US inflation leaves Waller’s half-point December hike in play

Core CPI rose 0.3% for a third consecutive month, matching the level Waller linked to considering a half-point December hike before the release.

US monthly headline inflation slowed in October, but core prices rose 0.3% for a third consecutive month, matching the level Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller had associated with considering a 50-basis-point December interest-rate hike.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics reported on Nov. 10 that consumer prices increased 0.4% from September, seasonally adjusted, after September's 0.6% rise. Core CPI, which excludes food and energy, rose 0.3%. The agency did not publish headline or core year-over-year rates.

That leaves a policy tension beneath the slower headline figure. In prepared remarks earlier that day, before the release, Waller said core CPI around 0.3% or higher would warrant considering a 50-basis-point December hike if the report resembled the preceding two monthly readings. He had leaned toward 25 basis points under his expected outlook.

October's core reading matches that approximate level. The comparison does not establish Waller's assessment after the release or a decision by the Federal Open Market Committee.

Prices continued to increase in October, even though the overall monthly pace eased. Energy rose 0.7%, following a much larger 5.2% increase in September. Food prices also increased, with the index rising 0.5% in October.

Shelter increased 0.3% and accounted for more than 30% of the monthly headline increase, according to BLS.

The core result shows why the headline slowdown offers only part of the picture. Removing food and energy leaves the same 0.3% monthly increase recorded in August and September. There was no corresponding improvement in that monthly measure.

Slope chart plots reported September and October seasonally adjusted monthly CPI rates on one linear 0%-0.6% scale. Dashed connector marks an editorial comparison with Waller's approximate core CPI condition, not causation or a policy trigger. Basis points describe the possible rate move, not CPI.

Monthly changes, not annual inflation. Waller's conditional personal remarks preceded the release; neither a post-release endorsement nor an FOMC decision is established. Most October annual comparisons, including headline and core, are unavailable.

Price changes also varied across categories. Recreation rose 0.8% and motor vehicle insurance increased 0.7%, while used cars and trucks fell 1.2%. Those differences matter when assessing a report that combines slower overall inflation with continuing pressure in several components.

Waller's remarks explicitly state that the views are his own and do not necessarily represent colleagues on the Fed Board or the FOMC.

Waller separately discussed personal consumption expenditures inflation, the measure targeted by the FOMC. The comparison with October's release concerns core CPI; the two measures should not be treated as interchangeable.

BLS did not publish October year-over-year changes for headline or core CPI. During the 2025 lapse in appropriations, the agency did not collect October 2025 survey data, leaving most series without the prior-year indexes needed for an annual calculation.

For Bitcoin readers, the immediate policy question is how officials assess persistent core inflation alongside the slower headline pace.

The next test is policymakers' post-release assessment and subsequent inflation evidence ahead of December. BLS has scheduled November's CPI report for Dec. 10 at 8:30 a.m. Eastern.

#5164. Coinbase Options Flag XRP as Top Volatility Bet With 9.7% Swing Signal: What’s Next?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. Cryptocurrency options markets are pricing in potentially significant price swings across major digital assets through Nov. 15, with XRP recording the largest implied move compared with Bitcoin, Ethereum and Solana.

In an X post, the Coinbase Markets X account shared 7-day implied moves for top cryptocurrencies. "Crypto options imply these 1σ moves through Nov 15," the Coinbase Markets X account wrote.

Crypto options imply these 1σ moves through Nov 15: $XRP ±9.7% $SOL ±9.3% $ETH ±8.5% $BTC ±6.0% $XRP carries the widest premium to its median 7d move (1.97×). Which market delivers? https://t.co/pGXf0eFh8K

According to the latest options data, XRP carries an implied one-standard-deviation (1σ) move of ±9.7%, slightly ahead of Solana at ±9.3%. Ethereum follows with an implied move of ±8.5%, while Bitcoin has the lowest expected range at ±6%.

The figures suggest that options traders are betting on a rise in potential volatility in XRP over the coming days. However, they don't indicate whether prices will rise or fall. They may, however, provide an indication of how much price movement is currently priced into options.

XRP is notable for another reason. "XRP carries the widest premium to its median 7d move (1.97×)," Coinbase wrote. This implies options markets are pricing in a much larger move than its average weekly move.

This premium makes XRP particularly interesting as traders assess whether the token can deliver the volatility currently reflected in derivatives pricing.

At the time of writing, XRP was down 2.59% in the last 24 hours to $1.06 amid a broader market selloff.

The consumer price index data for October came in hotter than expected. The CPI for October rose 0.4%, more than the 0.3% expected from economists polled by Dow Jones and down from the 0.6% growth in September.

A December rate hike now seems likely, with Fed Governor Chris Waller suggesting even a 50-basis-point move could be appropriate.

A 9.7% increase for XRP would potentially target $1.16, whereas a 9.7% decrease from current levels would see XRP drop to $0.95. In the coming days, the markets will closely watch which direction XRP will trend next, upward or downward.
