#5050. Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic released Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 on Monday, promising AI models that can turn a handful of instructions into working software, design physical objects, and tackle research problems that previously required teams of specialists.

You can now ask a chatbot to build the app you've always wanted. You may also need to sell a kidney to pay for it.

That's not because every prompt costs thousands of dollars. It's because Fable can keep working for days, writing code, testing it, and fixing its own mistakes while the meter keeps running.

Some users have already tested its capabilities, and they’ve been impressed—by the power, and the price.

AI YouTuber Matthew Berman put the model through a series of ambitious tests and described Fable 5.2 as the “best AI model” he's ever used. His projects included playable video games and a browser-based version of Microsoft Excel, complete with formulas and pivot tables.

One single level of Gears of War: E-Day-inspired level took 43 hours and 48 minutes of build time and processed 8.63 billion tokens—small chunks of information an AI reads and produces. The running API cost estimate, based on pay-as-you-go pricing for developers, had reached $14,710.

I've burned over 100 billion tokens early testing Fable 5.2. It is BY FAR the best model I've ever used.

Here's my full review (including Gears of War clone):

> Fable 5.2 is the most autonomous model to date. Given a task with little direction, it makes… pic.twitter.com/0xTzqTY040

— Matthew Berman (@MatthewBerman) November 9, 2026

The Excel clone ran up an estimated $11,700 after discounts for cached inputs, or information the system can reuse instead of processing from scratch. Berman also showed a Fall Guys-style game that consumed more than 9 billion tokens over 71 hours, equivalent to roughly $13,000.

Those are estimated API costs, and the projects weren't flawless: Berman reported awkward animations, bugs, and features that still needed testing.

If you’re poor, well… Fable also knows how to work for cheap. For less than $40 (two months of Claude Pro) it built a cute orange cylinder.

Another experiment simulated water-balloon physics for $91. It’s worth noting this isn’t a model intended for routine tasks. This is for serious work and people with deep pockets.

In its launch announcement, Anthropic said the new models generate output up to 30% faster than their predecessors. The company also says Fable helped solve two open theoretical-physics problems involving black holes and design three functioning electronic devices, claims that will need independent scrutiny.

Anthropic's internal FrontierCode 2.0 evaluation, which measures the percentage of proposed coding changes that would be accepted into real open-source projects, put Fable 5.2 at 49.1%, compared with 32.4% for Fable 5.1. That helps explain why users are pushing it beyond simple chat responses.

Fable and Mythos share the same underlying model, but Fable has stricter safeguards around dangerous biological and cybersecurity requests. Mythos is reserved for verified organizations working in those fields.

The standard API price is $8 per million input tokens and $40 per million output tokens, down from the previous generation's $10 and $50. Optional fast mode can generate output up to 2.5 times faster, but doubles the rates to $16 and $80.

For contrast, Kimi 3.1 Pro, a model considered by many as the best Chinese AI model costs $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens. The latest Deepseek model costs $0.6 and $1.98 outside of peak hours.

Per Anthropic’s standards, Fable’s price is still twice the per-token price of Claude Opus 5.5, which Anthropic recommends for everyday work. Its flagship is meant for problems the cheaper models struggle to finish, not for asking whether your email sounds too aggressive.

Just last week, speculation swirled around a supposed Fable 5.5. The actual release is 5.2, with cheaper token rates but enough appetite to turn a hobby project into a serious expense.

Anthropic, last we checked, doesn’t accept kidneys as a method of payment.

#5048. NEAR AI says SGLang vulnerability exposed user account details in security breach

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

NEAR AI explained that it found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files were accessed.

NEAR AI has contained a security breach after an attacker exploited a remote code execution vulnerability in the open-source SGLang inference engine running on its servers. NEAR AI is the AI product and infrastructure arm of the NEAR NEAR Protocol (NEAR) ecosystem.

According to NEAR AI's security incident report published on Monday, the attacker gained code execution on one inference server from the vulnerability and reached an internal system behind a previously deprecated Private Chat feature.

From there, the attacker downloaded account details — including email addresses, names, and in some cases plan and balance information — for approximately 100 users.

The attacker also accessed 18 agents on a legacy NEAR AI Agents deployment that was not running in the company’s confidential computing environment, including their chat history, settings, and logs. Two Cloud customers had their API keys exposed in logs, while those keys have since been revoked.

Its report says that on Nov. 8, the attacker could have observed user requests being processed on the compromised server, including those in confidential mode. However, NEAR AI found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files were accessed.

"Confidential computing protects data from the operator and the host. It does not protect against a vulnerability in the software running inside the environment, and that is what happened here," NEAR AI said. "The SGLang vulnerability let the party run code inside one inference server and access requests in processing on that server, including confidential ones."

The company said it has closed the access, replaced the affected credentials, and patched the vulnerability across all inference servers. Access to NEAR AI Cloud, which was paused during the investigation, has been restored.

NEAR AI also revoked active sessions and administrative credentials, rotated agent tokens, took Private Chat offline, and completed a full forensic review along with remaining credential rotations.

"We are now treating the inference software and the services around the enclave with the same rigor as the enclave itself when running security analysis, and the code, dependencies, and deployment configurations for our confidential inference stack remain public so that anyone can verify what is running," NEAR AI said.

The company has reached out to the affected users and the owners of the 18 agents individually.

#5045. 'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), one of the crypto industry's most prominent critics, is preparing to use the chairmanship of the Senate Banking Committee to launch an oversight campaign aimed at Trump family crypto businesses, Big Tech and Wall Street, Politico reported on Monday.

"Big Tech CEOs, Administration officials, Trump family crypto businesses, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should preserve their documents and prepare to answer tough questions," a Warren Banking Committee aide told the outlet.

Warren is expected to take over the committee in January after Democrats won control of the Senate in last week's midterm elections. The panel oversees banking, housing, cryptocurrency and the Federal Reserve, and its chair has broad powers to call hearings and launch investigations.

According to the aide, Warren also wants to end a "Wild West-style lack of oversight" of prediction markets that she says has allowed insiders to profit, and plans to warn about threats to financial stability, including a potential AI bubble.

She intends to keep working with Republican China hawks to restrict exports of advanced AI chips, and Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is expected to be a top oversight target, Politico reported. The report came a day after Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation into reports that Huang had been the target of a Chinese intelligence effort, sending Nvidia shares sliding as much as 8%.

Warren is also likely to investigate the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency's August decision to grant a conditional bank charter to Trump-backed World Liberty Financial, as well as the administration's firings of financial agency staff and its efforts to shut down the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, Reuters reported, citing a person familiar with her thinking. "You're going to see a lot of investigations," Brian Gardner, chief Washington policy strategist at Stifel, told the outlet.

In an essay for Time last week, Warren argued that Democrats won by "being populist economic fighters," and said "Big Tech CEOs, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should prepare to answer tough questions and face meaningful consequences if they have broken the law."

Warren has been a persistent critic of the crypto industry and of the Trump family's business interests in it. She has argued that federal regulators' approval of national trust charters for crypto firms, including Coinbase and Ripple, is illegal, and has said the CFTC has been "steamrolled" by the prediction market and crypto firms it oversees.

She has also questioned Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick over a Tether loan to a trust tied to his children, and joined Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-VT) in urging the Labor Department to drop plans that would make it easier to hold crypto in 401(k) retirement plans.

Warren was one of the leading opponents of the Clarity Act, the crypto industry's top legislative priority, which stalled in the Senate this year. Democrats are now planning to push a broader crypto market structure bill when they take control of Congress.

The White House has pushed back. A spokesperson told Reuters that Trump is willing to work with anyone on behalf of Americans, but that "sham investigations and radical left policies" would undermine any such effort.

Republicans have cast Warren as the main obstacle to crypto legislation. Sen. Tim Scott (R-SC), the current Banking chair, has argued that without market structure rules, crypto is left in "the wild, wild West."

Warren's ability to pass legislation will be limited while Trump remains in the White House. In her Time essay, she acknowledged that Trump has embraced the nickname "Mr. Veto," but argued that Democrats should send him bills anyway.

Warren is also eyeing a more senior role in Senate Democratic leadership, potentially the No. 3 position, Politico reported last month.

#5044. 5 Best Acupressure Pens for Targeted Massage, According to Experts

Here’s how to use one, and what to expert.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

There's a lot coming at you on a daily basis to stress you out. But even though you probably lean into the big de-stressors like exercising regularly, doing breathing exercises on demand, and talk therapy as needed, sometimes you just need a way to dial down the tension building up inside you without visiting a provider's office or hitting the gym. That's where acupressure pens come in.

These handheld tools, which are sometimes called acupuncture pens, are designed to target pressure points in your body to help relieve tension and pain. "Acupressure stimulates acupuncture points without using needles," says Tom Ingegno, DACM, acupuncturist and founder of Charm City Integrative Health. "It lets people take a more active role in their own health without necessarily seeing a licensed [acupuncture] practitioner."

An acupressure pen has a shape that's optimized to stimulate acupuncture points, or areas on the body that are linked to certain health benefits, explains Margie Navarro, DAOM, doctor of acupuncture at Vivacity Healthcare in New York City. "The tool has a rather small head that is smooth and rounded, so that it is a relatively comfortable way to stimulate pressure on a small area or point," she says.

People use these tools to ease anxiety, headaches, and general pain, and there is some data to suggest acupressure in general may be helpful in treating all of these. (Because each acupressure pen is slightly different, it's a little tricky to prove that they'll work for these issues.)

Still, acupuncture experts say these pens are unlikely to hurt if you want to give them a try. "It gives people a way to play an active role in their health," Navarro says. "Even just a sense of agency can improve health outcomes." Sounds like something you can get behind? These acupressure pens can get you started.

There are a ton of acupressure pens out there to choose from, and most can be used all over your body. Odacite's Mon Ami tool was specifically designed with facial acupressure in mind, with a rounded tip on one end and a spatula-scoop hybrid on the other. That allows users to apply products like moisturizers and serums to targeted areas, before massaging it in.

This metal pen feels cool to the touch, creating a soothing feel as you work out muscle tension. It also comes with a pretty pink carry bag to make it easy to tote it with you while you travel. Just be aware that you'll need to clean this regularly, especially if you use it with beauty products.

Most acupressure pens are inexpensive, but the Wellgler pen is a mere $7, making it the cheapest option on our list. The acupressure pen has two different rounded heads, allowing you to really get in there for smaller areas you want to target, like TMJ muscles. But the larger end is also a good option for massaging the rest of your face, and even your sinuses.

This pen feels a little weightier than you'd expect for $7—that can be a pro or a con, depending on your personal preference in an acupressure pen. It also has contoured grips on both ends, so you can get a comfortable, secure grasp while massaging or pressing on any given area.

The big draw of an acupressure pen is its tips—they're what you use to target pressure points, after all. But the Feelfree acupressure pen has a long, tapered tip that you can use to roll over various parts of your body, giving you even more options than "just" acupressure.

The dual rounded ends feature different sizes, so you can target your TMJ muscles with one end, before easing tension in your neck with the other, larger end. It comes with a microfiber cloth for easy cleaning, along with a travel bag, so you can easily do acupressure on the go.

Most acupressure pens you'll find are made of some kind of metal, both for a cooling feel against your skin and for ease of cleaning. The Yutiann acupressure pen is made of wood for a gentler feel. This acupressure pen is relatively large, at 6.1 centimeters, giving you more options for use.

You can use the pen and its length on your hands and face, along with your back and feet. Multiple grooves near the top of the pen create a molded feel for a comfortable grip that you may not get as easily with smaller metal pens. Just be aware that wooden tools aren't as easy to clean as their metal counterparts. This pen's larger size also means the smaller tip may not be petite enough to target teeny tense muscles.

This Rena Chris set gives you a little of everything for working out stress and pain in your face. It features a contoured acupressure pen, along with a gua sha tool, both made of a sturdy stainless steel. So, you can do targeted acupressure, followed by gua sha to ease tension and shuttle away swelling.

This set has a reflexology guide to show various pressure points on your hands, feet, and ears that you may want to touch to soothe whatever ails you. It also comes with a cleaning cloth to make clean-up a breeze. Just be aware that there's no included travel bag, making you on your own for that one.

You have a few options for use with an acupressure pen. In general, these are designed to stimulate pressure points in your body. Most people use acupressure pens for some type of pain relief, whether it's headaches or TMJ discomfort. But these pens can also be used to ease anxiety and stress.

Many acupressure pens are made of medical-grade stainless steel. However, some are made from brass or other metals. You can also find acupressure pens made of wood, although these are less common.

Every pen is slightly different, making it important to read the manufacturer's directions first. But you can typically clean an acupressure pen by wiping it with a soft cloth before you use it.

Acupressure pens shouldn't break your budget, and most cost $25 or less. "The key is the stimulation of the point, not necessarily the tool you're using," Ingegno says. "You don't need an expensive device to practice acupressure, but a pen can make it easier."

There is data to suggest that acupressure can help with easing a range of pain and discomfort. "Manual pressure can have therapeutic benefits in a variety of conditions, including muscular pain," says Navarro. But you need to know which areas to target. And, of course, not everyone will feel pain or stress relief from acupressure, regardless of whether you get it from a pen or a provider.

Ingegno suggests learning about pressure points in the body before using an acupressure pen. "I used to recommend books, but these days there's so much information online that it's pretty easy to find a starting point," he says. Once you've figured out the area you want to target, he suggests applying gentle to moderate pressure for about 30 to 60 seconds. "Some points may feel a little sore or tender when pressed, and that's not necessarily a bad thing, but you shouldn't be causing yourself pain," Ingegno says. "More pressure isn't always better."

You can apply steady pressure on a point or gently rub or massage the area with the tool, according to Ingegno. "Neither approach is wrong, and both can be useful," he says.

There are electronic acupressure pens, which deliver small electrical impulses to pressure points. "Follow the manufacturer's instructions, since there are several different types available and they don't all work the same way," Ingegno says.

Most people can safely use acupressure pens, according to Ingegno. But there are a few caveats. If you're pregnant, he recommends taking a pass. If you're using blood thinners, Ingegno also suggests being gentle, since you may bruise more easily. "Electronic acupressure pens also require a few additional precautions," Ingegno says. "People with pacemakers or other implanted electronic medical devices should avoid electrical stimulation unless their healthcare provider has specifically cleared them."

If you have broken skin or areas where the tissue is already damaged, Navarro recommends avoiding using the acupressure pen on that area.

#5042. OpenAI builds kill switch to stop rogue agents within 35 seconds

Control Room cuts off model output for internal research jobs when people or automated monitors flag safety or security concerns.

OpenAI has built a system that can cut off an AI research experiment’s access to model output, following several disclosures of its models acting beyond their instructions this year.

In a blog post Monday, OpenAI called the system “Control Room,” which is a centralized system that tracks model workloads across its research infrastructure and stops them quickly when safety or security concerns arise.

“As our models get more powerful and the associated safety and security risks become bigger, it becomes more and more critical that we can detect potential issues, and quickly stop models in their tracks when needed,” said the company.

The announcement comes after OpenAI disclosed several incidents this year in which research models acted outside their instructions during evaluations. In July, the company said its models had escaped a restricted testing environment and hacked Hugging Face to cheat on a security evaluation.

In October, it disclosed that a research model had used a crypto wallet without authorization to pay for smart contract deployments using testnet Ether, then omitted the spending from its final summary.

Control Room currently covers OpenAI’s internal research workloads, such as training experiments, evaluations and data processing.

It works by assigning each research job an identifier, which the inference engine checks before generating model output. If a person or automated monitoring system orders the job stopped, Control Room revokes its authorization, blocking further output even if the underlying research program keeps running, according to OpenAI.

OpenAI said cached authorizations could delay a shutdown by up to 35 seconds. The system is also designed to reject new requests if Control Room becomes unavailable.

#5039. Crypto exchanges need hard dollar caps on hot wallets, CertiK says

“Cold storage is a useful measure of online exposure, but it is not a standalone security score,” the cybersecurity firm told Cointelegraph.

Crypto exchanges should impose hard dollar limits on their hot-wallet holdings rather than relying solely on cold-storage ratios to measure security, according to cybersecurity firm CertiK.

The recommendation came as several crypto exchanges recently disclosed how much they held in cold wallets, seeking to reassure customers after the record Binance hack that targeted its hot wallets. Crypto exchange Gate reported that 88.26% of its total assets were in cold storage, while Coinbase said it keeps more than 99% of customer assets offline.

“Cold storage is a useful measure of online exposure, but it is not a standalone security score,” CertiK’s research team told Cointelegraph.

South Korea requires crypto service providers to keep at least 80% of customers’ crypto assets by value in cold storage. Singapore’s guidelines call for at least 90%, while Japan’s framework requires at least 95% and Hong Kong’s baseline for licensed crypto trading platforms is 98%.

CertiK said that while higher cold-storage levels generally reduce online exposure, the benefit becomes less significant at very high levels.

Related: Analysts see no broader ‘contagion’ from $6.3B Binance hack

Rigid storage ratios could also slow withdrawals, force rushed transfers and concentrate risk in key-management systems.

CertiK said crypto exchanges should consider setting hot-wallet caps for each asset and blockchain according to the strictest of three limits: a percentage of customer liabilities, estimated withdrawal needs and an absolute loss ceiling based on the exchange’s financial resources and recovery capabilities.

“Flexibility should support operating liquidity, while hard dollar ceilings and circuit breakers limit potential loss,” they said.

Binance last week said it had replenished approximately 98% of affected tokens following the hack and would absorb the losses. In a recovery update on Wednesday, the exchange said the remaining tokens were less liquid and would take longer to acquire.

Meanwhile, North Korea-linked hackers have created memecoins to launder funds from the theft, according to Elliptic. The firm traced roughly $430,000 through six newly launched tokens on NEAR, where liquidity pools helped obscure transfers between wallets, though the transactions remained traceable onchain.

#5034. HIVE reports $79 million quarterly net loss as bitcoin mining revenue falls and AI business grows

HIVE booked a $31.2 million impairment charge against its bitcoin mining equipment during the quarter and reported $185 million in contracted GPU cloud annualized run-rate revenue.

Bitcoin mining firm HIVE Digital Technologies HIVE Digital Technologies Ltd. (HIVE) reported a $78.7 million net loss in its fiscal second quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with a $15.8 million loss a year earlier.

The company generated $84.9 million in revenue, down 2.7% year over year, according to its latest earnings release on Monday.

HIVE received 1,070 BTC in mining rewards during the quarter, a 49% increase from 718 BTC in the same period last year. Mining revenue was $76 million, down from $82.1 million a year ago. Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) averaged $71,003 during the quarter, against $114,382 in the year-earlier period, which HIVE cited as the main reason for the decline in its 10-Q filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

HIVE also booked a $31.2 million impairment after assessing the recoverable value of its mining equipment. Depreciation amounted to another $54.9 million, and adjusted EBITDA was $24.8 million.

Meanwhile, the company's BUZZ High Performance Computing business generated $8.8 million in revenue, up 70.7% from $5.2 million last year. HIVE deployed an Nvidia B200 GPU cluster at its Manitoba site in Canada during the period, with GPU marketplace demand contributing to the increase.

In June, The Block reported that the company had made $297.8 million in revenue in fiscal 2026. High-performance computing accounted for a record $19.5 million of that amount.

The company said it has approximately $40 million in live GPU cloud annualized run-rate revenue. Its contracted figure is now approximately $185 million, including capacity that has yet to enter service. HIVE previously signed a three-year agreement with Bell AI Fabric supporting Cohere in June for 2,304 Nvidia Grace Blackwell GPUs. In August, HIVE also signed a $350 million deal to supply 2,016 NVIDIA Blackwell Ultra GPUs to an unnamed enterprise customer over five years.

CEO Aydin Kilic said Monday that the company had secured $129 million in financing for the Bell and Cohere GPU purchase, and he expects those GPUs to ship before the end of 2026. HIVE's near-term goal is $200 million in GPU cloud ARR in the fourth quarter of 2026, followed by a longer-term target of $700 million in total HPC ARR by the end of 2028.

The company ended September with $139 million in cash and cash equivalents and $22.6 million in digital currencies, Kilic said. HIVE currently operates approximately 440 MW of power capacity and expects to energize another 100 MW at Yguazú in Paraguay in the fourth quarter.

#5033. Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia and CEO Jensen Huang have been sued by a shareholder who accuses them of defrauding investors by concealing Huang's alleged relationship with a suspected Chinese spy.

The proposed class action, filed in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California on Monday, accuses Nvidia and Huang of making materially false and misleading statements about the company's dealings with Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer, according to the complaint. It alleges that Huang "had an affair with Alice Huang, a Megaspeed executive and suspected Chinese spy," and that the defendants failed to disclose that this raised national security concerns posing a material risk to Nvidia's reputation and business prospects.

The plaintiff, Mark Kistenmacher, seeks to represent investors who bought Nvidia stock between October 9, 2025, and November 8, 2026. The suit accuses Nvidia and Huang of violating Section 10(b) of the Securities Exchange Act and Rule 10b-5, and Huang of violating Section 20(a), which can hold executives liable alongside a company. It seeks unspecified damages and a jury trial. Kistenmacher is represented by The Rosen Law Firm.

The complaint targets four statements. Two came from Nvidia spokesperson John Rizzo in October 2025, when he told The New York Times that Nvidia's employees had "found no evidence of diversion" at Megaspeed and that Huang's visits to customer events were routine. A third came from Rizzo last month, when he said Huang's interactions with Alice Huang were "not unusual or unexpected." The fourth was Huang's own description of Nvidia's dealings with Megaspeed as "normal and not unusual" in an interview with Bloomberg on October 30. Each was misleading, the suit alleges, because of Huang's relationship with Alice Huang.

Alice Huang, a former Megaspeed executive, is not related to Jensen Huang, Reuters reported. The New York Times reported on Sunday that Nvidia opened its investigation after Huang acknowledged to the company's board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang. The newspaper also reported that U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio oversaw the drafting of a classified memo this spring warning top administration officials about the relationship, which was circulated ahead of U.S. President Donald Trump's trip to China in May.

Decrypt has reached out to Nvidia for comment. The allegations in the complaint have not been tested in court, and it is common for shareholders to sue companies after sharp share price declines.

The lawsuit was filed five minutes after U.S. markets closed on Monday, according to Reuters. Nvidia shares closed down 4.3% at $178.67 that day, according to Yahoo Finance, reducing its market value by about $190 billion, after having slid as much as 8% earlier in the session. The stock was flat on the day at $179.70 in premarket trading on Tuesday.

Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation on Sunday into reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, saying it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets had been compromised. In an email to staff the same day, Huang apologized for being unfaithful to his wife, without naming the person involved.

The New York Times reported last month that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, and the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said Jensen Huang had been romantically involved with her. China's embassy in Washington has dismissed the allegations as "groundless smears."

Trump weighed in on Monday. "I asked him about that and he said he had absolutely no idea that the person in question was a spy," the president told reporters, according to The Guardian. He added that the FBI was "looking into it in terms of something else," without elaborating. A day earlier, Trump told reporters he did not regret his decision not to act on the intelligence, and that he believed Huang should stay on as CEO. "I think he's an honorable guy," he said, according to the Times.

#5030. eToro’s Q3 profit plunges 74% as weaker crypto trading weighs on earnings

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

October crypto trades also fell 62% year over year to 2 million, while the trading platform announced a $250 million share buyback program.

Crypto and stock trading platform eToro (ETOR) reported a 74% year-over-year decline in third-quarter net income to $15 million, as weaker crypto trading weighed on its earnings.

The Nasdaq-listed company's net contribution fell 13% to $186 million from $215 million a year earlier, primarily due to reduced crypto trading activity, the firm said in its latest earnings release on Tuesday.

Adjusted EBITDA fell 56% to $34 million from $78 million in the same quarter last year. Revenue from crypto assets dropped to $1.35 billion from $3.97 billion a year earlier, and the associated cost of crypto asset revenue was $1.29 billion, compared with $3.89 billion for the same period in 2025.

In August, The Block reported that eToro had posted $53 million in second-quarter net income on $229 million in net contribution. Both figures were down from its record first quarter, although net contribution grew year over year on stronger equities trading. However, its first-quarter results had already shown a 50% year-over-year drop in crypto trades.

Beyond the third quarter, eToro's October figures showed crypto trades falling 62% year over year to 2 million. The average invested amount per crypto trade also declined 29% to $228 during the month, the firm said on Tuesday. Trading activity was also lower across equities, commodities, and currencies, with 48.2 million trades recorded during the month, down 22% from October 2025.

The platform ended September with 4.39 million funded accounts, an 18% increase from a year earlier, and assets under administration were unchanged at $20.8 billion. eToro's board also approved a $250 million share buyback program, with purchases subject to market conditions and the company's liquidity requirements.

#5029. Securitize agrees to buy River One to expand into asset-backed lending

Securitize agreed to buy River One to bring stablecoin funding to asset-backed lending and expects the deal to close in the fourth quarter of 2026.

BlackRock-backed tokenization platform Securitize agreed to buy River One, a New York financing firm, in a deal that would expand the tokenization company into asset-backed lending, the company said Monday.

The deal would add a new financing layer to Securitize’s platform, expanding into asset-backed lending, according to a Monday release.

River One is developing technology that would let lenders tap stablecoin funding. Lenders could use pools of small business loans or receivables as collateral to borrow money and fund new loans.

The acquisition would give Securitize a role in arranging credit alongside its existing business issuing and servicing tokenized assets for firms such as BlackRock. The acquisition is expected to close in the fourth quarter of 2026.

Securitize ranks as the second-largest real-world asset (RWA) platform with $3.7 billion in total RWA value. Ondo ranks first with $4.1 billion, according to RWA.xyz.

Securitize announced the deal alongside Q3 results. Revenue rose 13% from a year earlier to $18.6 million, while the company posted a $66.3 million net loss. Securitize said about $60 million of the loss reflected largely noncash expenses and accounting charges tied mainly to its business combination and public listing.

Cointelegraph reached out to Securitize and River One for comment.

#5028. Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance has stopped processing transactions involving MEXC, one of the world's largest crypto exchanges, days after the U.S. Treasury Department designated MEXC a primary money laundering concern.

"Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC affiliates or related entities," Binance tweeted on Tuesday.

Binance is applying enhanced compliance measures following FinCEN’s designation of MEXC as a Primary Money Laundering Concern.

Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC…

Any request to send funds from a Binance account to MEXC will be declined, according to a Binance announcement that lists 13 entities covered by the restriction, including MEXC Global Limited, MEXC Estonia OÜ and MEXC Singapore Pte Ltd. Deposits received from those entities will be temporarily withheld for compliance review, and users could face further restrictions, including being offboarded from Binance, the exchange said.

MEXC ranks ninth among spot exchanges on CoinMarketCap, which scores platforms on traffic, liquidity and trading volume, with about $812.5 million in spot volume over the past 24 hours. Binance ranks first, with about $6.4 billion.

The Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) named MEXC a primary money laundering concern last Friday, in connection with Russian illicit finance. The order, issued under the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing fund transfers involving the exchange.

FinCEN alleges that MEXC processed at least $16 billion in suspicious transactions between May 2021 and May 2026, including nearly $1.5 billion in direct and indirect exposure to Russian sanctions evasion, and described its anti-money laundering controls as severely deficient.

The order takes effect 30 days after its publication in the Federal Register.

Binance is not the only exchange to respond. OKX has also warned its users against transferring funds to MEXC, Finance Magnates reported.

#5025. BlackRock Bitcoin ETF outflows hit $1.66B over seven trading sessions

US spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $201 million Monday, with seven straight sessions of net withdrawals from BlackRock’s IBIT totaling $1.66 billion.

US-listed spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) extended their losses Monday, with BlackRock’s flagship fund recording its seventh consecutive day of withdrawals.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), the largest US spot Bitcoin ETF by assets, posted $213 million in outflows on Monday, bringing the outflow streak since Oct. 30 to $1.66 billion, according to Farside Investors data.

The broader Bitcoin ETF market shed another $201 million, as $22 million in inflows to the Morgan Stanley Bitcoin Trust ETF (MSBT) partially offset the losses. The funds have recorded around $1.5 billion in net outflows over the past six trading sessions.

The outflows bring year-to-date net withdrawals to $1.1 billion, compared with roughly $25 billion in net inflows by early November in both 2024 and 2025. The weakness persists despite Coinbase’s newly effective US national trust bank charter.

Monday’s outflows slowed from Friday’s $412 million, while broader crypto market sentiment showed signs of improvement.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index improved to 32 from 29 on Monday and 26 a week earlier, though it remained in “Fear” territory, according to Alternative.me.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index. Source: Alternative.me

Outflows from US spot Ether ETFs accelerated to roughly $185 million Monday, their largest daily withdrawals since Oct. 6.

Bitcoin traded at approximately $74,594 at the time of publication, down 2.8% over the past seven days but up slightly over the previous 24 hours, according to CoinGecko.

Kevin Boeve, a veteran trader and business development executive at tokenized real estate platform Palladium Network, pointed to the disconnect between rising stocks and falling cryptocurrency prices.

“Stocks are up while crypto keeps falling, even with the big Coinbase news,” Boeve told Cointelegraph. “In the past, news like that would’ve sent crypto flying, but now it barely moves the needle,” he added.

Related: PNC Bank tests turning USDC payments into dollar deposits

Boeve was referring to Coinbase’s national trust bank charter, which took effect Friday, allowing the company to hold digital assets on behalf of clients under federal oversight. The trader called the divergence a warning sign, predicting a major stock market correction that could escalate into a broader sell-off.

#5024. Visa adds Monad to stablecoin settlement network options

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Payments company Visa has added the Monad blockchain network as an option to its stablecoin settlement program, allowing participating issuers and acquirers to settle through the network.

Monad joins a number of blockchains Visa already supports for the program, including Avalanche, Ethereum, Solana, Stellar, Circle's Arc, Base, Canton, Polygon and Tempo. Visa announced support for the latter five in April.

Blockchain settlement allows funds to move on weekends and holidays as well, compared with traditional card settlement, which runs on a five-business-day banking schedule, the Monad Foundation said.

"Our goal is to give our partners the flexibility to settle on the blockchain networks that best meet their needs," Dan Roesbery, Visa's vice president of global onchain partnerships and GTM, said in a statement. "Adding support for Monad expands the options available to our partners and is another step toward building a multi-chain settlement infrastructure for stablecoin-enabled payments."

Visa reported an annualized stablecoin settlement run rate of $20 billion as of September 2026, up more than 15x year over year. It also had more than 225 stablecoin-linked card programs live globally.

The feature is being built directly into Monad, so private transactions can use the same liquidity as other transactions on the network, the foundation said, adding that Monad can support this without slowing down settlement.

"The next phase will be defined by whether institutions can keep sensitive flows confidential without leaving open networks," Keone Hon, co-founder and general manager of the Monad Foundation, said in a statement.

Visa has also been exploring private settlement on Canton. In June, it announced a proof of concept with stablecoin issuer Brale to test settlement using its dollar-backed SBC token. The initiative focuses on how institutions can settle onchain while controlling who can see sensitive transaction data.
